FAA Ground Stoppage Could Impact One Of Hollywood’s Busiest Weekends

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

UPDATED with FAA lifting the ground stop: The FAA’s nationwide ground stoppage has been lifted, but, coming on the eve of one of the entertainment industry’s busiest weekends, the residual effects could affect numerous entertainment industry events as organizers scramble to determine if attendees will be able to make it to the events.

One of the most high-profile events, the Academy of Motion Picture Arts and Sciences’ 17th Governors Awards, a must-attend for every A-lister in the industry with more than 225 invited guests for this year’s show, is scheduled for Sunday at the Ray Dolby Ballroom at Ovation in Hollywood. It features honorees Glenn Close, Floyd Norman, and Ridley Scott. Killer Films producers Christine Vachon and Pamela Koffler will receive the Irving G. Thalberg Memorial Award. With everyone in town, several events are planned around the Governors Awards, and attendees are scheduled for roundtables, Q&As, and other FYC events.

Deadline reached out to the Academy for comment, Watch on Deadline

Another big event that could be impacted this weekend is the Rock ‘N Roll Hall of Fame induction ceremony, Saturday at the Peacock Theater in Los Angeles. Phil Collins, Billy Idol, Iron Maiden, Joy Division/New Order, Oasis, Sade, Luther Vandross and Wu-Tang Clan are on the performers list, along with honorees Celia Cruz, Fela Kuti, Queen Latifah, MC Lyte, Gram Parsons, Linda Creed, Arif Mardin, Jimmy Miller and Rick Rubin.

Also scheduled for Monday in Los Angeles is the Critics Choice 9th Annual Celebration of Black Cinema and Television, but good news here – Deadline understands there will be no impact on the program. Hosted by Roy Wood Jr., honorees include Debbie Allen, Quinta Brunson, Chiwetel Ejiofor, Chris Rock, Lamorne Morris, Katori Hall, Jaafar Jackson, Anthony Mackie, Stephan James, Sophie Okonedo, Ato Essandoh, Quincy Tyler Bernstine, Arie Esiri and Chuko Esiri and Jaalen Best.

The American Film Market wraps up Sunday in Los Angeles. Foreign buyers and sellers set to fly home may be spending a longer time in L.A. than expected.

Also impacted is Vancouver BC airport, traditionally the 8 pm flight from Vancouver to LAX has been called the “Hollywood flight typically filled with film and tv industry-ites returning from a week’s working in British Columbia.

The industry also has two high-profile red carpets on Monday, The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping in New York, and The Further Mis-Adventures Of Cliff Booth, Quentin Tarantino’s follow-up to Once Upon A Time… In Hollywood, in Los Angeles.

The Hunger Games distributor Lionsgate told Deadline it is monitoring the situation.

Netflix also is hosting a screening Saturday for AMPAS members for The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth, in which Brad Pitt reprises his Oscar-winning role from Once Upon A Time.

Also impacted by the aircraft grounding is the Texas Book Festival, which takes place Saturday and Sunday (Nov. 14-15) in Austin. A number of attendees, including talent, heading to Texas were stranded at airports Friday trying to get there.

Dominic Patten, Anthony D’Alessandro and Pete Hammond contributed to this report.

AFM Dealmakers, Talent Agents and Hollywood Stars Left Stranded by Nationwide Flight Stoppage

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Federal Aviation Administration issued a halt on all U.S. flights on Friday at around 1 p.m. PT and didn’t lift the order until approximately four hours later. With the American Film Market, the Governors Awards and numerous FYC panels scheduled over the next several days, the stoppage left many Hollywood insiders grounded on one of the busiest travel weekends of the year.

AFM was hit hard by the standstill, and several events were canceled as dealmakers were preparing for their final days at the major sales function. Ryan Werner, president of global cinema at Neon, told Variety he was unsure if he could make it to AFM on Saturday for the studio’s party. He mentioned “our flights” when referring to travel plans, likely meaning several members of the Neon team were shut down on the way to Los Angeles. Related Stories 'Stranger Things' Prequel Spinoff 'Other Worlds' Ordered to Series at Netflix

One agency source told Variety that when the FAA shut down travel, their department broke out in a panic to see if anyone could still make their scheduled appearances at Saturday’s Governors Awards, which take place at the Dolby Ballroom at Ovation Hollywood.

“Once the news happened, everyone jumped on the phone to see if anyone is going to be able to make it to L.A. for the Governors Awards,” they said. “One agent has a client traveling through Europe with no idea how he will get back for his next things in the U.S., so they might have to hang out in Canada to at least be close.”

TMZ reported that “Baywatch” star Brooks Nader’s private jet was stopped in New York just before taking off for Louisiana. The outlet also shared a video of “Bachelorette” star Kaitlyn Bristowe stuck in an airport. Flava Flav and Morgan Fairchild both took to social media to share that their travel plans were affected. Variety also confirmed that the Sharpe Family Singers and actor Isaac Robinson Smith were all left stuck at the airport.

News of the flight stoppage broke around 1 p.m. PT. At the time, the FAA said the pause was “due to extraordinary circumstances and for reasons of safety.” Department of Homeland Security Markwayne Mullin later appeared on Fox News and clarified that it was a “telecommunications outage” that caused the backup and that there was no indication of a “nefarious attack” against the United States.

The FAA announced around 6 p.m. PT that the nationwide ground stoppage was canceled.

Tom Hardy's Ridley Scott Thriller Is The Ultimate Slow-Burn, And Season 2 Can't Come Soon Enough

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Cher Thompson is a Senior Staff Writer at Screen Rant, where she covers everything from explosive reality TV moments to cast interviews about the twists and turns of scripted dramas. With a background in creative writing, marketing, and teaching, Cher knows how to spot both the storytelling genius and the guilty pleasures in pop culture. Her work blends wit with insight, making readers feel like they’re chatting with a friend who just happens to know way too much about TV.

Some TV thrillers make their biggest impression through constant action, while others take their time establishing an atmosphere like Tom Hardy's Ridley Scott slow burn series, which will be returning for a second season in the near future. While a flash-in-the-pan thriller series can make waves in the short-term, shows that build out their stories and establish a long game can be more interesting to viewers. When all of the elements of a show with a slow burning plot come together, it can be delicious for the audience as a reward for their time spent watching and faith in the writers.

This slow burning quality is what makes one of Hardy's most distinctive TV projects worth revisiting, especially as the series has a second season coming in the future. The historical thriller, which premiered in 2017 and has remained in the public conversation since, had some struggling with its deliberately slow pacing, while other viewers praised the way that it took on dense storytelling. The series, created by Hardy and his father, Chips, and executive produced by Ridley Scott, follows James Delaney (Hardy) a mysterious adventurer returning to London in the early 1800s to claim an inheritance and confront issues within his father's business.

Although it's been nearly a decade since the first season of Taboo aired in 2017, the show has long been a topic of conversation, especially with the series' second season being confirmed by Hardy himself in recent press. While the wait's been long, the journey to Taboo season 2 will surely be well worth the wait.

Set in 1814, Taboo follows Hardy's James Keziah Delaney, an adventurer who returns to London after years in Africa left him presumed dead back at home. Word of his father's death brings him back to claim what remains of his family's shipping empire, but James' inheritance isn't exactly straightforward, as the East India Company has their own business interests in the company. Other powerful figures also have their own reasons for keeping Delaney from taking control of the family business, as his return opens old wounds and changes family relationships. As the reasons behind his father's passing are called into question, Delaney's world slowly begins to fall apart.

While the show does operate as a period piece due to its timing, it isn't always a conventional one. The version of London that unfurls in Taboo is grim and complicated, focusing on a more corrupt depiction of the city than the reality of what early 19th century London was like. With Hardy's performance anchoring the show, Taboo is able to move within its slow rolling story because his spectacular performance. On top of Hardy, the ensemble cast of the series does a phenomenal job in depicting a fascinating mix of characters in the most difficult of circumstances.

After a renewed burst of attention because of a streaming resurgence, Taboo is more in demand than it's ever been before. The most frustrating part of Taboo's legacy has been the fact that it had a stellar first season and seemed like a series that was determined to continue, but has yet to air another season after its 2017 debut. With Delaney and his companions leaving London on the search for their next adventure, the challenges that came with creating another season of Taboo didn't seem too intense when the first season came to a close. Thankfully, Hardy himself recently confirmed there was more to come.

Despite the unusually long wait between seasons, Taboo does seem like it will be back for another season at some point, though there's no filming dates or release window to speak of. Though a second season isn't guaranteed to arrive anytime soon, the series does seem to be a lock for another season in the future. Taboo has demonstrated with ease that its long-form storytelling can have a lasting impact, so the amount of time between seasons may simply create more material for the show to work with. Taboo's return will be another win for Hardy.

Kamal Khera Named New Canadian Consul General In Los Angeles, Will Meet With Studios

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Canadian Prime Minister Mark Carney has appointed a new Consul General in Los Angeles, who says she hopes to inspire a renewed sense of unity between Canada and the U.S.

The Honourable Kamal Khera was introduced as Canada’s Consul General in Los Angeles on Thursday night at a reception at Canada House, the Consul General’s residence in the city’s Hancock Park neighborhood. A longtime Member of Parliament in Canada, Khera held multiple roles in the federal Cabinet under Carney, and predecessor Justin Trudeau, including serving as the Minister of Health, Minister of Seniors, and Minister of Diversity, Inclusion and Persons with Disabilities. The 37-year-old Brampton, Ontario native now heads across the border, where she will be representing Canada across Southern California, Arizona and Nevada. Related Stories Oscar Predictions: Production Design — Could Something as Innovative and Cool as 'Backrooms' Find Love? Dear Oscars: Don’t Let Laika and ‘Wildwood’ Fail. There Is No Backup Stop-Motion Studio Waiting in the Wings

In prepared remarks Thursday, Khera said her first order of business will be to address the growing distrust between Canadians in her region and the U.S., a divide which has been spurred by President Trump’s ongoing trade war, along with comments he made last year about Canada becoming the “51st state.” With almost 50,000 Canadians living in Southern California, and thousands more across Arizona and Nevada, the three states represent the largest contingent of Canadians in the U.S. (in the 80s, Los Angeles was said to have had the fourth-largest Canadian population of any city in North America).

Khera stressed that there is a history and bond between the two countries that should not be forgotten. “Canada and the United States share a partnership that runs deep, built over generations through shared interests, common security, economic ties, and friendships between our people,” Khera remarked. “The United States has been, and will remain, our closest friend, ally, and partner,” she continued, acknowledging that “we are living through a period of considerable change. But one thing my years in public life have taught me is that relationships are not defined by a single moment,” she said. “They are built over time, through trust, mutual respect, and a shared commitment to working together. And that is the spirit I hope to bring to my time here.”

Khera will likely be pressed to address the so-called Hollywood Exodus, which has seen a number of film and television productions move north of the border (Canada currently offers a 16% labor credit on top of a 30% incentive in the province of Manitoba). Of course, Canada has also seen its share of success with homegrown exports like the surprise Bell Media hit, “Heated Rivalry,” which was later acquired by HBO Max.

While Khera joked that friends have already been texting her for “Hollywood gossip,” the Consul General is expected to sit down with various interest groups and studios in the coming months. Still, she was quick to note that California, Arizona and Nevada have long been places to foster collaboration, not only in arts and entertainment, but in technology and industry as well. “Whether it’s connecting Canadian businesses with American investors, advancing collaboration in aerospace and defense, supporting our creative industries, or bringing together innovators and researchers, there is tremendous potential in what we can accomplish together,” she said. “And I want our Consulate to be an active partner in turning those opportunities into lasting relationships and meaningful results.”

Prior to her diplomatic appointment, Khera served nearly a decade in the Canadian Parliament in Ottawa. First elected in 2015 at the age of 26, she was one of the youngest women ever elected to the House of Commons. Before entering public life, Khera worked as a registered nurse in oncology.

Khera replaces outgoing Consul General Zaib Shaikh, who departed last November.

7 Best Moroccan Rugs (2026)

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Moroccan rug is one of the easiest ways to add texture and intrigue to any room. Originally made to protect floors against the harsh cold of the Atlas Mountains, we see these lush, woolly carpets as a staple in the modern design world because of the range of color and style options available. The rug type has become synonymous with traditional undyed cream colors, vibrant jewel tones, and geometric patterns. It’s no wonder they pop up across tons of our home tours—like this artful Brooklyn studio and floral Victorian house—and have become a favorite among artists like Claudio Bravo and celebs like Patrick Dempsey.

Boucherouite, the hand-weaving technique employed to make these rugs, was created by semi-nomadic Amazigh tribes, and the style can be found in abundance in the busy souks of Marrakech. Now, with many direct-to-consumer and vintage Beni Ourain-inspired styles on the market, the perfect Moroccan rug is just one click away. With all the options at your disposal, it may be intimidating to know where to start. Fear not, we’ve rounded up a few top picks below along with advice on how we’d style them.

Give your guests the coziest welcome with this Azilal rug from Revival Rugs. Its long, narrow design makes it ideal for tight entryways. And while the oblong diamond pattern speaks to geometric patterns you’ll see across traditional Moroccan rugs, the pink tassel is a niche, colorful detail that won’t overpower surrounding decor. This is an authentic hand-knotted Moroccan rug, with wool locally sourced from the Atlas Mountains. Because of its small size, you may also consider hanging it up to add a textural element to your wall decor.

Break up the monotony of home office decor by placing an eclectic rug under your desk setup. While the soft grey and subtle design still create a distraction-free workspace, the cheeky oxblood hues add a touch of personality to make you smile between emails and Zoom meetings.

You don’t have to get a custom carpet straight from Marrakesh to have a high-end rug. The Saira rug from Quince is a handwoven option at an affordable price. The geometric motif is heavily inspired by Moroccan design and the medium-pile, hand-spun wool is designed to feel plush under your feet.

Calling all maximalists and color drenchers. The rich, vibrant colors from this rug come from a family of third-generation dyers, and the irregular shapes of the boxes infuse a quirky texture to whatever room they are placed in. Either have this rug as the standout piece in a room that is otherwise neutral, or pair it with other green pieces like this couch from Ellison Studios to engulf yourself in a verdant indoor forest of your own making.

Even though this muted rug may look understated at first glance, it brings visual interest thanks to the grid design along the border. The motif takes inspiration from Mediterranean tile patterns, in case you were wondering. We can see this as a soft, elegant layer underneath your dining table. Water-resistant and machine washable, this rug can survive stains and spills without making you bat an eye.

Why should the adults have all the fun? Bring whimsical design to your child’s bedroom or playroom with this Moroccan area rug from Benisouk. We have a feeling that the high pile will feel comfy for the kids to run around on and durable enough to withstand a little wear and tear from playtime. The dreamy creamsicle hues of this rug can match youthful decor, but the abstract minimalist design is elevated enough to be a piece that grows with them through the years.

If you find yourself longing for sleek, beige interiors—or are in the midst of remodeling your home to mirror an Aman resort—look no further. This handmade sheep’s wool rug is a minimalist dream. The neutral cream brings a layer of calm, while the organic shapes offer a wabi-sabi element that may be necessary in a pristine white room.

Trump doesn’t plan to raise Nvidia, spy issue during China trip: U.S. officials

📅 2026-11-14  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

President Donald Trump is not expected to raise allegations of a Chinese intelligence operation involving former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang during his upcoming meeting with Chinese President Xi Jinping, senior U.S. officials said Friday.

Officials also said that Elon Musk, the CEO of Tesla and SpaceX, will accompany Trump on the trip to Shenzhen, China, for the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation summit on Nov. 18 and 19.

The White House, earlier Friday, said Trump plans to have a three-way summit there with Xi and Russian President Vladimir Putin.

Jensen Huang's relationship with the suspected spy, Alice Huang, who is not related to him, has raised concerns that a Singapore-based company where she was an executive diverted restricted Nvidia chips to China.

"The situation with Nvidia should not really have any bearing on the trip," an official said on the press call, speaking on the condition that they not be identified.

"In all of our discussions and negotiations with the Chinese, we do not negotiate American export controls ... and we don't expect it to figure at all in any of our conversations with the Chinese while we're there."

Asked if Trump would raise the allegations against Alice Huang to Chinese leader Xi Jinping, the official said, "The focus of a meeting with President Xi, at least on the economic side, is going to be about implementation of the trade agreement."

"When it comes to some of these national security matters and examinations and investigations that are going on in the U.S., [that is] probably not something that we're going to read the Chinese in on at this moment," the official said.

Another official said, "I don't expect the president to raise it — the Alice Huang issue."

China has called the allegations against Alice Huang baseless.

Huang resigned this week as Nvidia's CEO after damning reports that he had an affair with Alice Huang, who was an executive of Megaspeed, a cloud-computing company that is being eyed for possible facilitation of sending billions of dollars worth of Nvidia chips in violation of U.S. restrictions on those chips.

The scandal has rocked Nvidia and raised questions about whether the Commerce Department, which is led by Howard Lutnick, has been aggressive enough at blocking advanced chips from ending up in China.

During Friday's call, a reporter asked why Musk was going on the trip with Trump.

"I don't want to speak for the president on exactly why Elon's going," an official said.

"Obviously, he has tremendous business interests in the U.S.-China relationship. He's a big part of our national security," the official said. "There are numerous reasons, given the engagement that the president is going to have, where I think Elon would be of tremendous benefit, just even as an advisor to the president."

Personal Investments • Re: Please Explain VTIP

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

ICPI is made of TIPS with 0-1 year maturity. Not zero interest rate risk, but less than VTIP. It will soon be 1 year old. During that time the return if you invest dividends has been 4.49% with a max drawdown of .34% which lasted 3 weeks. That is better than VBIL, USFR, XHLF and XONE. VBIL and USFR don't really have any drawdowns to speak of if that is highly important. USFR is the only one of those with a higher Sharpe ratio.

https://testfol.io/?s=5YRWW54mnVQ

In inflation adjusted terms ICPI is the only one of the group with a positive return of .29% as shown in this link.
https://testfol.io/?s=hqGBlBd09z3
If you do a price comparison (after adjusting for known inflation and seasonality) of individual TIPS to T-bills, TIPS almost always sell at a small liquidity price discount (aka a liquidity premium) in the last year of their life. If you really have no plan to sell before maturity, it is close to free alpha. The premium is pretty consistent, but it works both ways, get better return than a T-Bill, but if you have to sell it early you lose on price.

Note, for folks buying/selling these, be careful in the day before/of/after each CPI release. The price adjusts over the trading day, not instantly, but the newly known inflation is always priced in within a trading day or so. For some reason, yields are often lower on TIPS maturing in April as well. One of the market's mysteries.....

Assuming ICPI holds to maturity, it is a good case for holding that period. VTIP also holds to maturity, but as noted term risk is higher. I've been using VTIP for years as the core to my TIPS holdings along with a longer-duration TIPS ladder. I don't really sell from VTIP though, I just spend the dividends for the most part so I have little if any stress about term risk.

I will note that any TIPS with less than 6 mos. to maturity has almost no unknown inflation coming since the known inflation is always between 15-45 days depending on the time of the month. Really, in the last year, TIPS provide very little inflation protection on an absolute $ basis.

Live Service Fatigue Claims One More Popular Shooter

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Covering the video games industry since 2017, with experience in news, articles, and reviews. Currently a writer at DualShockers and gaming magazines.

Another ambitious online shooter is biting the dust, and this time, it seems that players won't even get the chance to return for one last update.

GFA Games has officially announced today that PIONER, its post-apocalyptic MMO inspired by titles like STALKER and Fallout, is shutting down. The studio is ending development, canceling future updates, and preparing to take the game's servers offline after failing to secure the funding necessary to keep the project running.

In an announcement shared on social media, the development team explained that maintaining PIONER required additional financial support, but they couldn’t find any. The studio had spent the past few months negotiating with existing and potential partners, hoping to find an investment deal that would allow development to continue and preserve the team.

Unfortunately, those discussions didn't lead anywhere. According to the developers, GFA Games no longer has enough money of its own to continue working on the game or maintain its server infrastructure.

After years of near radio silence, Fairgames has been canceled and its staff redistributed throughout Sony games.

“We were unable to agree on the funding needed to continue the project. The studio no longer has sufficient funds of its own to continue development and keep the servers running.”

Aleksandr Nikitin, CEO of GFA Games, even addressed the community, expressing his disappointment over the decision and apologizing to players who purchased PIONER expecting more updates. He explained that the team had envisioned a long future for the project and had been trying to find a way to keep both the game and its developers together.

The recent announcement effectively puts an end to those plans. Since PIONER is an online-only experience, shutting down its servers also means players will no longer be able to access the game once the infrastructure is taken offline.

It's quite an unfortunate outcome for a game that spent years building up expectations before finally arriving on Steam Early Access in December 2025. The post-apocalyptic MMO promised an enormous world filled with dangerous mutants, exploration, survival mechanics, and multiplayer activities, which initially attracted some attention on Steam, reaching a peak of more than 1,600 concurrent players around launch. However, that interest quickly faded. By September, its 24-hour peak had dropped to just 11 players, according to SteamDB.

GFA Games has officially announced today that PIONER, its post-apocalyptic MMO inspired by titles like STALKER and Fallout, is shutting down.

Unfortunately, its launch was plagued by technical issues, and these numbers show that the shooter struggled to maintain a consistent player base in the months that followed. Now, GFA Games revealed that it really was struggling to turn its project into a sustainable live-service experience.

Ratchet & Clank: Ranger Rumble is being shuttered before it could even reach worldwide launch status.

It's always disappointing to see an ambitious multiplayer project disappear like this, especially when players have invested money and time into its world. But the studio thanked everyone who supported PIONER, reported bugs, created content, and helped build its community, while acknowledging that it couldn't deliver the future originally promised.

For now, the development team hasn't provided a specific date for when the servers will go offline. Still, with development and updates officially ending, PIONER's days are numbered. And while the game may not have reached the audience its developers hoped for, its shutdown is another reminder of how difficult it can be to keep an online-only game alive when the players and financial support aren't there.

Community outcry over the sunsetting of Destiny 2 has made Bungie reconsider the next steps.

Jensen Huang Is Out: How Bad Is This for Nvidia Stock?

Going Green: Miss Monique Debuts Her Nature-Centric Biorhythm Show in the U.S. This Weekend — With Bigger Plans For America to Follow

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

"I feel like my relationship with the U.S. is having an amazing start," the Ukrainian producer says ahead of her Nov. 13 show at Brooklyn Pacha.

On a recent afternoon, Miss Monique was in her studio in Lisbon when she got the news: Her upcoming headlining show at Brooklyn Pacha was sold out.

The Ukrainian producer, known for her progressive house productions and signature green hair, has already played key U.S. festivals like Ultra and EDC Las Vegas. But news of a sold-out headlining set hit different.

“I’m very appreciative of the U.S. crowd for the support,” the artist born Alesia Arkusha tells Billboard over Zoom from her home in Lisbon. “To bring your own show, that’s a completely different thing… you have more responsibility. But we’re sold out, so I think it’s another great sign of our relationship with the U.S. market.”

Certainly it points to real momentum toward Miss Monique — already famous throughout the European club scene — raising her profile in the U.S., which she cites as a major goal for 2027. “I would say I’m at the very beginning,” she says of breaking in the States. “For me, the U.S. crowd and market is completely different from everything I’ve done before.”

But with a sold-out show in one of the country’s key dance markets, she acknowledges with a smile: “I feel like my relationship with the U.S. is having an amazing start.”

She’s arriving with something special to offer: Biorhythm, the immersive audiovisual show she debuted at Tomorrowland’s Freedom Stage in July and brought to Argentina last month. (She next brings it to France later this month.) The show’s imagery weaves together the industrial and natural worlds — jellyfish, forests, iron structures — as a subtle meditation on what gets lost when technology crowds out the organic world.

“I started understanding that maybe I have a small power to remind people to be protective of nature,” she says. “I don’t want to give people a lesson, but Biorhythm is that reminder that we shouldn’t forget about nature.”

The concept is personal. Miss Monique grew up in Ukraine surrounded by parks and open land, and says she’s felt nature receding from her life as she’s moved through the churn of touring during her rise over the last 15 years. Biorhythm is her attempt to hold onto something she finds sacred, pairing it with the music she finds equally profound. “I love to live next to nature,” she says, turning her camera to show the forest outside her window. “Even here, I try to find places where I can sit and see something peaceful.”

Finding this peace hasn’t been easy. When Russia invaded Ukraine in February 2022, Miss Monique says she “woke up in bed at 4:00 a.m., because of the explosions.”

After the war started, she moved from Ukraine to Portugal and continued producing her signature progressive house and touring the world behind it, while her friends and family lived amid war. That dissonance has been painful. “My heart is broken,” she says. “Until the war is over in Ukraine, I cannot fully enjoy this life. I have amazing people around me; I’m based in a fantastic country. But I know my home is going through hell.”

In the war’s early days, she would often cry when audience members held up Ukrainian flags or offered words of support. She’s since “learned how to keep my emotions inside,” but the weight of the situation is never far from her mind.  ”I still always have that mark on my heart,” she says. “It’s the same for every Ukrainian.”

She takes solace in music, which she says with gravitas “is also medicine for me” as much as it is for those who see her play. “Music saved my life so many times,” she continues. “It saved me emotionally in the hardest moments. Music has always been there for me.”

She’s had a lot of new music to speak of this year, with her latest single “Undertone” with Belgian producer Paradoks out today. It follows “Beauty in Us,” her September collaboration with English pop legend Robbie Williams. The two first connected in 2023, when Miss Monique remixed a song from Williams’ electronic trio Lufthaus. She says working with him again on a full track “was an absolute honor.”

This spring, Miss Monique intends to spend one or two months living in the U.S., not just touring through it, as she works to raise her profile in the country.  “When we travel, we are huge fans of exploring, not just coming in and out,” she says of her and her partner. “I want to feel how people live, the culture, the mentality. I want to understand what it’s like to be based there.”

She’s already started doing her homework. In 2024 she made a trip to the Grand Canyon, where she recorded one of her signature outdoor livestreams. Standing at the canyon’s edge as the sun set behind her, she was moved to tears by the beauty. “I never even dreamed that I would be there one day,” she says. “That stream was one of the best of my life.”

It’s fitting that one of her biggest shows in America arrives with a nature theme. Green, after all, has long been her signature color. What started as teenage hair experimentation eventually became her brand, which was cemented when she tried going back to her natural hair color and fans revolted.

“They were fighting with me,” she says with a laugh. She called her stylist the next morning. The green has stayed ever since, spreading from her hair to her visuals to the cascade of green heart emojis that follow her across social media.

“Step by step,” she says, “I realized green is my color.”

45% of US Independent Arts Publications Face Existential Crisis, Report Finds

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

In addition to declining funding, the survey identified a disturbing correlation between censorship concerns and financial precarity.

Nearly half of independent publications covering arts and culture in the United States said their business models will not be sustainable without “profound changes” to the current funding landscape, according to a report from Critical Minded and the Dorothea and Leo Rabkin Foundation released this week.

The new survey notably identifies a disturbing correlation between censorship concerns, including external pressure from advertisers and sponsors, and financial precarity. The 41% of respondents who said they faced free-speech pressures or threats in the last two years were more likely to report funding declines, throwing into stark relief how what outlets choose to cover — or not — may determine whether they can pay staff and contributors, or keep the lights on altogether.

“Support for Arts and Culture Journalism: A Survey of Independent Publications in the United States” polled 141 independent outlets that cover the arts on issues impacting the field and strategies used to address them. The anonymized respondents are based in 34 states and the District of Columbia, with the highest concentration (38.2%) in the Northeast, and reach an average annual audience of about half a million.

Fisher Island Penthouse Sells After $12.2 Million Price Cut—Becoming the Private Island’s Second-Priciest Sale Ever

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A stunning seven-bedroom penthouse at Palazzo Della Luna on Fisher Island, which originally hit the market in Miami Beach for $50 million in June 2025, sold on Oct. 28, 2026, for $37.8 million.

Even with a discount of $12.2 million, or about 24%, off its original asking price, the sale is the ultra-exclusive island’s second-highest residential sale ever. The island's top record-breaking sale was a five-bedroom condo that sold for $40 million in 2021.

The seller was represented by Sam Gaita of The Corcoran Group, and the buyer was represented by Andrew Holtz of Compass.

According to The South Florida Business Journal, the seller was “ALJ Properties USA LLC, managed by Alicia L. Jarrett and Camilo Miguel Jr. with the same address in Miami as Mast Capital, where Miguel is founder and CEO.” The penthouse last changed hands for $23.75 million in 2021.

Built in 2019, the sophisticated 9,852-square-foot home has luxurious finishes, including gallery-style walls, wide-oak flooring, and a Boffi kitchen with double Miele and Sub-Zero appliances. The sale works out to $3,837 per square foot.

Floor-to-ceiling windows surround the unit, and retractable glass walls open to 4,500 square feet of outdoor terraces with panoramic views of the Atlantic Ocean, Biscayne Bay, and the Miami skyline.

The lavish primary suite has a fireplace, lounge area, dual walk-in closets, and a fluted marble bath with sweeping 180-degree views.

The condo also has a 2,500-square-foot rooftop terrace for entertaining, with an outdoor kitchen and its own private pool.

“Without question, the private rooftop terrace with a swimming pool is the most impressive feature,” Gaita said. “This is the only ‘lower’ penthouse on Fisher Island offering a private rooftop with a pool, which is an extremely rare feature throughout all of Miami.”

There are also separate staff quarters and garage parking for two cars.

“Fisher Island is an ultra-exclusive, private country club island accessible only by boat or helicopter,” he noted. “This 216-acre private island offers a private beach club, yacht club, multiple dining venues, tennis, pickleball, padel, spa and more, reserved for members and their guests only.”

Gaita said what ultimately drove the record sale was “attracting the buyer who best aligned with and appreciated the one-of-a-kind features the property has to offer.”

He also noted that its exclusive location “on a private island comparable to a country club, isn’t something that can be easily replicated and serious buyers understand that value."

"Fisher Island has always attracted buyers who value privacy above everything else. Sales such as this put the island in the same conversation as Indian Creek Island and Star Island for exclusivity," Gaita added.

Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Affairs, probes and a concert video: how 10 U.S. CEOs left their jobs

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

These 10 CEOs who left over affairs or relationships show how personal conduct and board scrutiny can end a top executive's tenure

Nvidia $NVDA CEO Jensen Huang has admitted that he was unfaithful, and he has not left Nvidia, according to The Economic Times. The publication built its list of 10 American business leaders around that admission. Each of them left the top job at their company after an affair or a relationship became public or was investigated. Huang is the contrast, not a member of the list.

The pattern says less about private morality than about corporate governance. A chief executive answers to a board, and the board answers to shareholders. When a CEO's personal life runs into a company's code of conduct, a board's questions tend to center on disclosure, judgment and risk.

A relationship with a subordinate can raise concerns about favoritism, conflicts of interest and the fairness of promotions. A relationship kept hidden raises a separate question. Did the person at the top follow the same rules that apply to everyone else?

The cases are not uniform. Some involved a relationship with an employee or a colleague. Patrick Byrne's exit from Overstock and Jide Zeitlin's exit from Tapestry did not stem from relationships with employees. Some departures followed a company investigation. Brian Dunn, for example, resigned as Best Buy $BBY's CEO while a misconduct investigation was underway. Andy Byron's exit from Astronomer followed a Coldplay concert video.

The common thread is narrower. In each case, a relationship or an affair became part of the story of how a CEO's tenure ended. Read together, the accounts of these CEOs who left over affairs or relationships show how differently boards, companies and executives respond when personal conduct becomes a corporate matter.

The list covers Harry Stonecipher, Darren Huston, Brian Krzanich, Jeff Zucker, Jeff Shell $SHEL, Per-Olof Loof, Patrick Byrne, Andy Byron, Jide Zeitlin and Brian Dunn. Their companies include media businesses such as CNN and NBCUniversal, retailers such as Best Buy and Overstock, and the fashion company Tapestry. The slides do not follow a strict timeline. Each one covers who the executive was, what came to light and how the company responded.

Harry Stonecipher is one of 10 American business leaders whom The Economic Times lists as having left their companies after an affair or relationship became public or drew an investigation. He held a chief executive role when he departed.

His board service also reached outside the executive suite. A filing with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission listed him as a director of PACCAR, Inc.

The same filing described his academic background. He earned a bachelor's degree in physics at Tennessee Technological University.

Darren Huston left the chief executive post at his company after an affair or relationship became public or was investigated, according to The Economic Times.

Brian Krzanich left his post as chief executive after a relationship with an employee came under scrutiny. He is one of the 10 executives The Economic Times names in its list of business leaders whose tenures ended after an affair or relationship came to light.

Jeff Zucker left his role leading CNN after a relationship became public. The Economic Times counts his exit among 10 cases of American chief executives whose tenures ended once an affair or relationship surfaced or was investigated.

Zucker's professional ties to Allison Gollust go back to NBC Universal. Gollust served as chief of communications there. Zucker was president and CEO of NBC Universal during that period.

CNBC reports that two employees described Zucker as a hands-on manager inside CNN. In their account, he took part in decisions about which guests appeared on air. He also weighed in on the graphics that ran on screen and on which stories the network covered.

Jeff Shell $SHEL left his role running NBCUniversal over a relationship. He is one of 10 American business leaders on The Economic Times list of executives whose exits followed an affair or relationship that became public or drew an investigation.

Comcast $CMCSA President Mike Cavanagh took over Shell's senior team and led NBCUniversal on an interim basis. Comcast Chief Executive Brian Roberts set out that arrangement in a note to employees.

Per-Olof Loof left his post as chief executive of KEMET. The Economic Times includes him on its list of 10 leaders of American companies whose tenures ended after an affair or a relationship became public or drew an investigation.

Patrick Byrne's departure from the top job at Overstock did not stem from a relationship with an employee. His case centered on a romantic relationship outside the company that he chose to discuss in public.

Byrne set out his reasons for leaving in a letter to shareholders. He tied his resignation to a decision he made in July to speak publicly, according to The Salt Lake Tribune.

That public account covered two subjects. One was his part in investigations into Russian interference in U.S. elections. The other was his romantic involvement with Maria Butina, who was described as an alleged Russian spy.

In the letter, the trigger Byrne named was his choice to go public about both matters, not the relationship on its own.

Overstock named company executive Jonathan Johnson as interim CEO after Byrne resigned.

Andy Byron left his post as chief executive of Astronomer after his relationship with a colleague became public. CNBC reports that a big screen at a Coldplay concert in Boston on a Wednesday showed Byron embracing Kristin Cabot, Astronomer's chief people officer.

Byron ducked out of view as soon as the camera landed on the pair.

A concert attendee filmed the moment. The video went viral.

Astronomer is commercializing Astro, an open-source data operations platform. Over the weekend after the concert, the company said it would start looking for a new CEO.

Jide Zeitlin left his post as chief executive of Tapestry, the fashion company, after a relationship became part of the story of his exit. His case did not involve a relationship with a Tapestry employee.

The Economic Times places Zeitlin on its list of executives who left after an affair or relationship became public or was investigated. He and Overstock's Patrick Byrne are the two people on that list whose departures did not center on a relationship with an employee.

Tapestry's board then had to settle who would lead the company and its board. It chose Susan Kropf, who had been the company's lead independent director, to chair the board of directors.

The company also said it was starting a search for a permanent chief executive. That search was open to both internal and external candidates.

Brian Dunn left the chief executive job at Best Buy $BBY after a relationship came under scrutiny. The Economic Times includes him among 10 American business leaders whose exits followed an affair or relationship that became public or drew an investigation.

Dunn resigned abruptly as chief executive. His resignation came after Best Buy began investigating allegations of personal misconduct against him.

The resignation did not end the inquiry. A Best Buy spokesman said the investigation remained open after Dunn stepped down.

Tripadvisor Reverses Merger of Experiences Teams, Cuts Cruise Critic Staff 60%: Scoop

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Enter your email for a complimentary article + exclusive offers.

Photo Credit: Tripadvisor's Matt Goldberg was interviewed by Skift Research's Seth Borko at Skift Global Forum on September 19, 2024. Skift

There's often a lot of hype about the strategic benefits in a restructuring. Tripadvisor admitted its November 2025 restructuring didn't work.

Tripadvisor has unwound a 2025 restructuring that combined its “things to do” and Viator experiences teams under one roof. The two businesses are back to separate teams, similar to how they were organized before the November 2025 changes, according to a source familiar with Tripadvisor’s operations.

Under the new structure, Tripadvisor’s things to do business will have its own team. That business mainly generates leads for Viator, the company’s main experiences brand and the focus of its investment. Viator’s experiences operations will have a separate team.

In a November 4 internal email to employees, sent after an all-hands meeting at which outgoing CEO Matt Goldberg announced a company-wide restructuring, Tripadvisor President Kristen Dalton wrote: “Experiences will be singularly focused on the Viator and Bokun brands with a focus on growth.” Bokun is Tripadvisor’s back-end reservations technology business for experiences operators, not a con

Tripadvisor has reversed the November 2025 restructuring that merged its "things to do" and Viator experiences teams, returning to two separate organizations with, as President Kristen Dalton wrote to employees, "far less overlap between them and fewer shared services." The company told investors the combination "has not proven out as we expected": over the past 12 months, experiences booked through Viator and partner channels grew in the low teens while the Tripadvisor channel declined by high single digits, and third-quarter experiences revenue fell 3% year over year to $284.4 million. Tripadvisor said November 5 it would cut 425 employee and contractor roles in its legacy Hotels & Other segment — about 60% of roles dedicated to hotel metasearch, display and sponsored ads, flights, cruise, and car rentals — with about 150 moving to Viator and a net reduction of roughly 275 positions, or around 17% of the workforce according to the Boston Globe. Cruise Critic, which sources said is profitable, is shrinking by 60% to around 20 employees, with all but one editorial staffer gone. The restructuring is expected to save about $40 million a year against $15 million to $20 million in charges, and it lands amid a leadership overhaul: CEO Matt Goldberg steps down in February, CFO Michael Noonan leaves December 31, and Dalton becomes interim CFO on January 1. Activist investor Starboard Value disclosed a 9% stake in July 2025 and gained four of 10 board seats in March; Tripadvisor has said no option is off the table for monetizing Viator.

CNFinance Announces Change of Auditor

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

GUANGZHOU, China, Nov. 13, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- CNFinance Holdings Limited (NYSE: CNF) ("CNFinance" or the "Company"), a leading home equity loan service provider in China, today announced the change of auditor, effective November 13, 2026. HTL International, LLC ("HTL") has served as the Company's independent registered public accounting firm since December 2024. HTL declined to stand for re-election to serve as the independent registered public accounting firm for the audits of the Company's financial statements for the financial year ending December 31, 2026. The decision was not a result of any disagreement between the Company and HTL on any matter of accounting principles or practices, financial statement disclosure, or auditing scope or procedure. The audit committee of the Company's Board of Directors has approved the change of auditor and appointed Guangdong Prouden CPAs GP ("Prouden") as the Company's independent registered public accounting firm for the fiscal year ended December 31, 2026.

The appointment of Prouden was made after a careful and thorough evaluation process and has been approved by the audit committee of the Company's board of directors.

Prouden succeeds HTL, the Company's previous independent registered public accounting firm. The reports of HTL on the Company's consolidated financial statements for each of the fiscal years ended December 31, 2024 and 2025 did not contain an adverse opinion or a disclaimer of opinion and were not qualified or modified as to uncertainty, audit scope or accounting principle.

During each of the fiscal years ended December 31, 2024 and 2025 and the subsequent period through November 13, 2026, there have been no disagreements between the Company and HTL on any matter of accounting principles or practices, financial statement disclosure, or auditing scope or procedure, which disagreements, if not resolved to the satisfaction of HTL, would have caused HTL to make reference to the subject matter of the disagreements in its report on the consolidated financial statements, and there have been no "reportable events" as the term is described in Item 16F(a)(1)(v) of Form 20-F.

The Company is working closely with HTL and Prouden to ensure a seamless transition.

The audit committee would like to express its sincere gratitude to HTL for its quality of services rendered to the Company over the past years.

CNFinance Holdings Limited (NYSE: CNF) ("CNFinance" or the "Company") is a leading home equity loan service provider in China. CNFinance, through its operating subsidiaries in China, conducts business by connecting demands and supplies through collaborating with sales partners and trust companies under the trust lending model, and sales partners, local channel partners and commercial banks under the commercial bank partnership model. Sales partners and local channel partners are responsible for recommending micro- and small-enterprise ("MSE") owners with financing needs to the Company and the Company introduces eligible borrowers to licensed financial institutions with sufficient funding sources including trust companies and commercial banks who will then conduct their own risk assessments and make credit decisions. The Company's primary target borrower segment is MSE owners who own real properties in Tier 1 and Tier 2 cities and other major cities in China. The Company's risk mitigation mechanism is embedded in the design of its loan products, supported by an integrated online and offline process focusing on risks of both borrowers and collateral and further enhanced by effective post-loan management procedures.

GTA 6 Owner Take-Two Is Not Interested In Selling Itself After EA Goes Private For $55 Billion

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Recently, the gaming giant Electronic Arts was taken private in a $55 billion sale to an investor consortium led by Saudi Arabia's Public Investment Fund. Could GTA 6 owner Take-Two be the next big company that gets acquired? Don't bet on it, CEO Strauss Zelnick says.

Speaking to David Rubenstein, Zelnick said Take-Two's business results have proven that it "can do very well as an independent company."

He said Take-Two's business is already "at scale" and healthy enough financially with a "terrific balance sheet," as he put it.

"We are not in a place where we need to be part of a bigger organization to be competitive. And in the entertainment business, scale matters," he said.

Zelnick said companies that do less than a billion in annual revenue might have issues with developing, marketing, and distributing games at scale. Not only that, but companies that don't generate a lot of money might not be able to survive the realities of the creative business, in which sometimes your product flops.

"But once you're over a billion dollars in annual revenue, and we're way beyond that point. Our guidance is $8 billion to $8.2 billion this fiscal year, additional scale doesn't seem to help you succeed."

In early 2008, Electronic Arts privately pursued a buyout of Take-Two, but after the GTA and BioShock company rejected it, EA went public in an attempt to get a deal done. Later that year, EA abandoned its plans to try to buy Take-Two after the GTA company said it was worth more and EA refused to increase its bid.

In 2019, it was rumored that Sony was attempting to buy Take-Two, but that was ultimately deemed to be unconfirmed market chatter and not a formal bid.

Take-Two has a market cap of more than $42 billion, and that is expected to grow in the year ahead, so it would seemingly take a lot of money to convince Take-Two to sell. Going forward, Zelnick has teased that new GTA games could release even faster, and that could be a positive for the company's financials.

GTA 6 launches on November 19 for PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series X|S. Preloading is underway now, and that's good news because the game has a massive file size.

FAA issues nationwide ground stop citing communication issues affecting ‘multiple’ air traffic control facilities

📅 2026-11-13  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Federal Aviation Administration issued a nationwide ground stop on Friday afternoon, disrupting hundreds of flights, after reporting communication problems affecting "multiple" air traffic control centers.

The issues were related to communications and radar issues, according to an internal message from Alaska Airlines, which was seen by CNBC.

A ground stop halts flights at their departure point.

"The FAA issued a nationwide ground stop for all U.S. airports following communication issues impacting multiple Air Route Traffic Control Centers," the FAA said.

The agency said in a statement that "FAA technicians are investigating the issue."

Inbound international flights will be managed on a case by case basis, the FAA said.

Nationwide ground stops are extremely rare. In January 2023, the FAA halted flights because of issues with a pilot alert system. Before that, traffic was halted nationwide after the Sept. 11, 2001, terror attacks.

How The Developers Of '007 First Light' Nailed The Feeling Of Being James Bond Through Its Cars And Vehicles

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

As a massive fan of the James Bond franchise, my main hope going into "007 First Light" wasn't that it would have characters and plot points I recognized or have all the right bits of fan service — I just wanted the game to evoke the same emotions that the Bond movies do, to really feel like I was being Bond when playing. Luckily the game delivered those feelings in spades. IO Interactive, best known for the "Hitman" franchise, created one of the best pieces of James Bond media ever, and a big part of "First Light's" success is down to the vehicles you get to drive as Bond and how they're woven into the main story.

To learn more about the game's development, and the creation of the new DLC missions that feature the Aston Martin Valhalla, I hopped on a call with Rasmus Poulsen, the game's art director, and Dominic Vega, audio director and game director of live content/missions. "We knew from the get-go that we wanted as wide a range of driving experiences as we could manage to squeeze in the game, and of course that means everything from the unexpected to the hoped for," said Poulsen, and man did they deliver.

SPOILER ALERT: While I won't be talking about major plot or character spoilers in this story, I do spoil all of the various vehicular scenes and moments, so if you haven't played or finished the game yet, bookmark this page and come back to it later.

The first excellent car moment comes in one of the very first missions, when Bond is going through initial training to join the reborn 00 program. At a facility in Malta, Bond gets thrown the keys to a 2006 Aston Martin DBS, the same car seen in "Casino Royale" and "Quantum of Solace." It's described as "a classic" by one of Bond's compatriots, who adds it's "seen some real-life action too back in her day. Still a gorgeous drive." She's not lying — the DBS is totally banged up, covered in duct tape and zip ties.

Bond proceeds to race around a course, smashing through boxes and jumping down staircases. Despite how realistic the cars look and sound the handling is definitely on the arcade side of things, but it's also very fun. You're encouraged to do handbrake turns and always keep the throttle pinned, and enough actual skill and good driving is needed to do well in this mission and subsequent ones.

It's not a very long sequence, with Bond quickly moving on to other parts of training, but it's a great way to get us car people excited at the beginning of the game. This particular choice of car this early on is a great nod to our outgoing Bond on the silver screen, too. Bond movies have always had car chases, but it wasn't until the Daniel Craig films that the chases really leveled up, becoming grittier and more realistic and more exciting, sometimes the most blockbuster-y set pieces in the entire movie.

A few missions later, Bond and his fellow 00 trainees have to infiltrate a chess tournament at the Grand Carpathian Hotel in Slovakia, and they drive up in a 2007 Jaguar XJ, with Bond dressed as a chauffeur. This is where the game really nails the Bond feeling, with the regular cars and seemingly mundane scenes, and it was this aspect of putting cars in the game that Poulsen and Vega enjoyed the most. "The Bond franchise is not only about the low end and the high end, it's also about the sleeper cars. It's about getting the car people to go, 'I see you,' and it's for us to go, 'we see you too.' The middle of the pack there is really exciting to me," Poulsen said.

"Each of the Bond movies, the best ones, are a cohort of bands. Think of the curation of the cast of vehicles in the movie," Vega added. "We're making an 18-hour experience, we want to make sure that the cast of vehicles gets the nod [from fans]. It gets the 'ah OK, I see what you're doing there, oh you're doing a twist here, oh it's 'Living Daylights.'" The development team was in contact with Aston Martin and Jaguar Land Rover from the very beginning, and they knew they wanted to include a big variety of cars. "There's partnerships that you have to do with Bond, right, it's not Bond without certain vehicles in the mix, but then you still always want to surprise, you want to 'and' the expectation. The player should absolutely have their fantasy met, 'and'. I think it's really nice when people in the car world notice that," he said.

In the Slovakia scene, the player calmly drives the XJ down a winding mountain road toward the hotel. You don't drive fast, or take any shortcuts, or go off the set path; it's all about setting a scene. While driving the XJ, Bond and his crew are talking about the mission and giving you more backstory, like in a transitional movie scene. Instead of making something like this a cutscene, "First Light" lets you act it out. It's like the vehicular equivalent of a "walk and talk," if you know your movie-biz slang. You can take your time on the drive, which is especially nice now that a photo mode has been added to the game. (Everything you see in this story was captured by yours truly.) With how much of the game is straight-up action, it's really nice to have these moments of levity in between that add more depth to the characters.

Poulsen says the development team visited the JLR headquarters in Gaydon, getting tours of the heritage collection and its restoration facility, to figure out what vehicles they might want to use in the game. There was everything from classics to prototypes and rarities like the XJ220, plus things that aren't even out yet. "We walked around discussing, 'hmm, we need something that's not what gets the front page, what is it going to be?' We looked at the XJ of that particular year; it has a story of old money rather than flash, it's not so old that it's vintage, it's just in-between," Poulsen said. They pointed it out to Jaguar, saying "we want that one," and the company obliged. It is a perfect spec, too, one of the later facelifts in green over cream with those lovely five-spoke wheels.

Another part of the game sees Bond and his mentor heading to Mauritania, with the level starting off in a kitted-out Land Rover Defender. You get to drive the SUV across the gorgeous landscape, even doing some light rock crawling, before coming upon a city built inside of a ship graveyard that's run by the pirate king Bawma, played brilliantly by Lenny Kravitz. It's again used as a conversation piece, setting up the scenes to come. Bond gets back in the Defender for another exploring sequence a little later, and after a series of events including a lot more fighting, he gets rescued with the help of a great Series II pickup.

It's not all mundane drives, though. Later in the mission that starts with the XJ, after chaos ensues at the hotel, Bond has to chase after someone who's just hopped into a second-gen Jaguar XKR. He does so by breaking into and hotwiring a Bahama Yellow 1970 Aston Martin DBS, the same model used in "On Her Majesty's Secret Service" (and the same color as the DBS from "The Persuaders"). There's a great moment of Bond cheekily flirting with a mysterious woman who hops into the car with him, and you're off.

Again, it's a pre-set route, but there are a bunch of fun shortcuts like swerving into a muddy field to avoid logging trucks, jumping over canals and driving through a farmer's market. You have to avoid other traffic and contend with other obstacles, and it can actually be a little tough at times. In one climactic moment, Bond drives the car onto and over a transport truck, using it as a ramp to get huge air. Who among us hasn't dreamt of doing that hundreds of times? Adding to the fun is a great damage model, with the DBS getting really beat up by the end of it even if you try and drive safely.

The car chase ends, but Bond is immediately thrust into a firefight at an airfield, which, of course, leads to him dodging explosions to hitch a ride on an escaping cargo plane. To do so, he takes control over one of those airport stair trucks, and jumps onto the wing of the plane. Then he fights a bunch of bad guys on the wing as the plane takes off, and eventually it leads to him freefalling from the plane, avoiding debris while trying to find a parachute — and you're in control pretty much the whole time. It's awesome, and really embodies that unique Bond feeling.

Like the "Hitman" games, "First Light" is all about using your environment to your advantage, and there's a lot of moments where a vehicle is part of that environment. In parts of the game where more baddies are arriving in cars, you can shoot at them, causing them to leak gas or oil and become easier to explode. Some vehicles can be hacked, with Bond able to set off car alarms or remotely activate gadgets like smoke screens. "Plenty of movies you see a car gadget that you never get to see [used in] the movie, so that was a little bit of a head nod there," Vega said.

The two best vehicular sequences in the game don't feature cars at all, though. Or at least not ones you're driving, since you do get to smash up plenty of others. At one point in the game, Bond escapes a gala by commandeering a garbage truck and wreaking havoc through the streets of London, and then later on he takes control of a giant mining truck and drives it through a quarry in Vietnam to, again, save his friends and escape the bad guys. While both sequences are small parts of much longer missions, they're absolute blasts to play through, some of my favorite moments of the game for sure, and they feel totally Bond. Just as you might realize, "oh hell yeah, I think Bond is gonna get in that tank" while watching one of the films, I was sitting on my couch thinking, "oh hell yeah, am I about to drive that thing?"

Vega said the two sequences came about in very different ways, with the mine truck being more organic. "The level's getting put together, and somebody puts a prop in, and then I think there is that incentive in game development, and especially in Bond, where you just want to say 'yes' to the player. Like you're rolling downhill, and someone throws something into the snowball, and instead of rejecting it you keep going." You see a bunch of these mining trucks earlier on in the mission, the game teasing what's about to come without you even knowing it yet. "The mining truck there being part of establishing the space, the place, and then ever so slightly you go, 'guys, well, let's go,' because you embody the mind of the character," Poulsen said of the scene being thought up on the fly.

"[That's the thing of First Light] being a catered experience. It's not open world, it's not everything, it's not all the vehicles; it's a specific subset of vehicles. That means it's interesting for us, that we can lure you forward with this truck and all these things, and have you go 'ah, yeah,' you got what you wanted. Rather than have a plethora of vehicles in front of you and you go 'OK, cool, which one?' No, the one we chose," said Poulsen. "You get what you wanted, but in a way that you wholly couldn't imagine or didn't imagine — unless you're a Bond fan, of course, in which case maybe you do imagine it."

The garbage truck, meanwhile, was a much longer road to get to. Vega talked about how resourcefulness is one of Bond's key character traits, one that differentiates him from other secret agent characters, and that's baked into the level designs from jump. Another one of Bond's best qualities is how he'll smash straight through an obstacle and keep on going. Which he does in this sequence quite a lot. "It was always in the script that he's going to get into this bin lorry in this really unexpected fashion. I think Craig embodied that, I think Brosnan had a bit of that, I think Dalton had a bit of that — put two dots on a map and draw a straight line, that's how James Bond got there, and that is a really cool moment of expression," Vega said.

What makes the garbage truck escape especially cathartic is how the mission starts: with an upset Bond sitting in traffic in a Range Rover SV, having a conversation with his boss. You can't even side-swipe traffic, with the game displaying a parking sensor warning on the screen and pulling the car back into the lane. "It's such a Londoner inside commentary there. He's frustrated, he's been told to stand down. We stick you in a powerful fantasy vehicle like the Land Rover and you can't open it up, you're just running errands. It's putting you in that headspace," said Vega, "so when we finally put you behind the wheel [of the garbage truck] an hour and a half later it's just like go go go go through the wall!"

One thing the game doesn't let Bond drive is what would traditionally be the hero car. In fact, the game rubs it in your face a bit. In a mission fairly early on in the game, you visit Q in his lab and have a conversation in front of a covered-up car that's being worked on by robots. The car is clearly the Aston Martin Valhalla to any slightly trained eye, and Q refuses to let Bond drive it even when it gets finished. He's not even a 00 yet, of course he's not getting to drive Q's souped-up supercar, and it's being built for a different agent anyway. "It kind of puts you in the shoes of Bond," said Vega, "who's like 'I wish you'd give me this on a mission!' That's always Q and Bond's relationship, it's a bit of restraint from Q where he gives you the gadgets you need, he's not giving you the keys to the castle."

Well, Bond will be Bond, so in one of the game's final missions, there's a scene where Bond gets those proverbial castle keys. To escape a siege on Q's lab he climbs into the Valhalla, fires up its machine guns and rockets, and does donuts around the room, killing bad guys and blowing basically everything up. You do actually get to control the car for this scene, but it's pretty brief, leading right into a cutscene that has a few comedic moments involving the car and its built-in voice assistant. It's a satisfying moment, since for hours after the Aston Martin is first seen, you've probably been thinking "man, I really hope we get to do something with the Valhalla."

The team began working on the game a little before the Valhalla was production-ready, receiving the CAD files from Aston so they could get started on it and figure out how to Bond-ify the car. "One of the artistic pillars for the project is that dichotomy between downplayed credibility and outlandish adventurous over-the-top-ness. We need to combine those two things," said Poulsen. Since the game uses a third-person camera view where you're looking forward from behind the character, and thus behind any vehicle, they decided against putting weapons in the headlights or front end of the car, where you wouldn't see them. The Valhalla has such a short, low front end there wasn't much to work with anyway. "We went with a design that would utilize the body and the shapes in an interesting way, and it made sense to put all the weaponry on the back," Poulsen said. "The principal concept artist did a bunch of designs with the CAD model, where we were experimenting with opening up the panels like a beetle or folding them in and out."

When they sent their ideas back to Aston, explaining the why and how behind their designs, the Aston team simply gave them the thumbs up. "Rather than being like 'no actually, you have a radiator there, and this and there's pipes and the thing and the valve,' they didn't give us a hard time because I think they could see that we had tried to some degree respect the negative space and interior space and all that stuff in the car," Poulsen said. The final design features the Valhalla's rear quarter panels opening out horizontally at an angle to reveal a giant auto-aiming machine gun mounted on an arm on each side. "We got that wonderful interplay and a really playful yet rather credible sense of weapons folding out. I'm sure it wouldn't necessarily be doable like this, but [Aston] didn't have a hard time with us wanting to do that, so it was a very smooth relationship."

That couldn't be it for the Valhalla, so the "First Light" team has released a few "Valhalla Protocol" time trial tactical simulation missions as DLC that let you really wring out the Valhalla on a newly designed course, either with or without weapons and obstacles engaged. When you're using the machine guns and rockets to take down obstacles, drones and other vehicles, it's definitely more outlandish than Bond movies usually are, but it fits right in with past Bond video games and reminds me of other excellent titles like "Spy Hunter." Even in strictly time trial mode it's pretty tough to achieve one of the best time targets, since you've still got to contend with all sorts of things on the road, like ramps that you obviously will want to jump off.

"At IO, we believe that shipping the game is the first step. We tell you the story we want to tell, but there's so much more that comes after that. We knew long, long ago that the best possible moment for the Valhalla was the climax of the techo-wizard experience. You've just done all this awesome stuff in Q lab, you see everything that Q built for you, it was always going to be racing around in donuts because you don't want to end that on a chase," Vega said. The final pursuit of the villain is very personal, so a climatic car chase wouldn't have felt right, but because the player now had Valhalla expectations from the start, Vega said they always planned to design post-launch content where you get to actually drive it.

It's a great redemption for the Valhalla, which has had a rough start to its time being featured in the Bond franchise. Aston revealed the concept version of the Valhalla in early 2019, and it originally played a bigger role in Craig's final Bond film, "No Time to Die," which started filming soon after the concept's reveal. That movie was supposed to come out in April 2020, but it ended up being delayed until September 2021 — a couple months after the production version of the Valhalla had been revealed, with a very different design and powertrain compared to the concept. In the final cut of the film, you briefly see the concept sitting in the background of Q's lab, and that's it.

As for what might make it into the next Bond movie, "First Light" could give us a little hint. In one scene when you're being escorted out of that ship graveyard by Bawma, Bond walks right past a bright blue Jaguar Type 00 concept. "Visiting the JLR design studio as a car nerd was just a dream come true, it was so cool, and we got to see the prototype of the Type 00, which was incredible," Poulsen said, "It was impressive to be there and see how that works. Walking past the clay model, it was just cool." JLR has provided Land Rovers and other cars to Bond movies for most of Craig's run, but I'm sure that its heavy involvement with "First Light" is a sign that it wants some new Jaguars to be featured in the next movies. I mean come on, can't you see James Bond driving the production Type 01? At least someone should, whether it's a villain or a Bond girl or hell, even M.

I couldn't get off the phone without asking Poulsen and Vega to tell me their favorite vehicular moments in the Bond film series, whether it be a major chase or a more nerdy throwaway moment. "I want to give a shoutout to the start of 'Quantum of Solace' just because it's so visceral and moody and cool," Poulsen said. "It's not as fun as the fun ones, it's more violent and hardcore, but damn that flows well. Or rather, it doesn't flow, it's much too staccato to flow, but ooh it's so good."

That is a great answer, but Vega holds the correct opinion, because his favorite is also my favorite. "Mine is 'GoldenEye,' but it's also because I think the dialogue plays such a role. It's a car chase where the villain is the Bond girl, but she's not having the conversation with him, he's having the conversation with the girl in the car," Vega said, referring to Bond's passenger, an MI6 employee named Caroline who is supposed to be psychologically investigating him. "I think it's the interplay; it's like a gunfight and it's a gadget fight and it's a car chase, all with just dialogue and driving. And it's low stakes, it's just having fun on the mountains. It's such an homage to the Bond girl outrunning Bond in a casual environment."

That's just one of a few great chases in that film. "The scale is so impressive, and it's the same thing [as the game] right, you go from high-precision handling, the fantasy of being the most capable person in the world, to driving a tank through a wall," Vega said, referring to one of "GoldenEye's" best moments. "I love the goofy vehicles of Bond a lot," he added. "I love the gondolas and I love the hovercrafts. It just brings out the boyish spectacle that makes me giggle."

Boyish spectacle is the best way to put it, and that's what "First Light" really gets right. Yes, the cars and traditional chase scenes are excellent, but what makes this game feel so undeniably Bond are the unexpected moments. We've got the holidays coming up, and you've got a week before GTA VI comes out, so if you haven't played through "007 First Light" yet, there's no better time to do so than the present. Though I guess you've got all the time in the world.
